## Open questions reported by researchers
- (pk) Primary full texts of Coskun et al. Cell Metab 2022 (single-dose PK) and Urva et al. Lancet 2022 (multiple-dose PK) could not be opened (Elsevier/Cell Press blocked access). Tmax ranges therefore come only from secondary reviews (12-72 h single dose; median 12-49 h multiple dose). The accumulation ratio and the exact single-dose half-life were not verified against the original papers.
- (pk) Retatrutide-specific time to steady state was not found in any source that could be opened. The 4-5 week figure comes from class labels (tirzepatide 4 weeks; semaglutide 4-5 weeks). With a ~6-day half-life a similar timeline is expected, but that is an inference.
- (pk) Absolute bioavailability of retatrutide and any injection-site differences (abdomen vs thigh vs upper arm) are unpublished. The dedicated study NCT05959096 (SC sites plus an IV arm) finished in July 2024 with no posted results. No published source states which body sites the phase 2/3 trials used.
- (pk) No population-PK publication for retatrutide was found. The only popPK numbers are the Cav,ss values posted on ClinicalTrials.gov for NCT04867785.
- (pk) Human ADME/excretion: the [14C] study NCT05757531 (completed June 2023) has no posted results, so retatrutide's breakdown pathway and excretion route are unknown. One small anti-doping study found no drug or metabolites in urine.
- (pk) Molecular weight: the only figure found is a computed value (4731) from PubChem. No WHO INN list, Lilly document or regulator page stating the full-molecule molecular weight could be opened. FDA GSRS lists 4091.546 Da, which appears to cover only the unmodified peptide chain.
- (pk) Human energy-expenditure (calorie-burning) data for retatrutide are unpublished; study NCT06313528 completed Aug 2025. GCGR-driven energy expenditure is shown only in mice.
- (pk) Whether the glucagon receptor is present in human fat tissue and the human heart is disputed between reviews (IUPHAR 2026 vs Diabetologia 2023).
- (pk) No retatrutide data on what happens after stopping. Withdrawal trial NCT06859268 is expected to finish around April 2028. All stopping data given are class data (tirzepatide SURMOUNT-4; semaglutide STEP 1 extension).
- (pk) Pharmacokinetics in kidney impairment (NCT05611957), liver impairment (NCT05916560), Japanese and Chinese participants (NCT04823208, NCT05548231), and a device comparison (NCT06003465) were completed but no published results were found.
- (pk) No official washout period for retatrutide (for example before pregnancy or surgery) was found in any trial or regulator document. The 56-day blood detection comes from a 4-person, uncontrolled study using self-sourced product.
- (pk) Retatrutide's percent albumin binding and volume of distribution are not published in any source that could be opened.
- (pk) Lilly says it will file a Biologics License Application (BLA) rather than an NDA for a 39-amino-acid peptide. No source opened explains why.
- (pk) TRIUMPH-1 (NEJM, Sept 29 2026) and TRIUMPH-2 (Lancet, per Lilly) full texts were paywalled or not opened. Only the abstract and press-release figures were verified.
- (pk) investor.lilly.com and lilly.gcs-web.com blocked automated access, so Lilly releases were read from PR Newswire copies and the SEC 8-K. No report of an actual FDA filing or of approval by any regulator was found as of 2026-10-07.
- (trials) TRIUMPH-1 heart-rate change by arm (and when it peaked) could not be verified: none of Lilly's TRIUMPH-1 releases report heart rate, and the NEJM full text/supplement (10.1056/NEJMoa2604169) returned HTTP 403. The same gap applies to TRIUMPH-2, -3, -4 and TRANSCEND-T2D-1: no phase 3 heart-rate data were found in any source opened.
- (trials) The NEJM TRIUMPH-1 per-arm adverse-event table was not opened. The dysesthesia (5.1/12.3/12.5 vs 0.9%) and AE-discontinuation (4.1/6.9/11.3 vs 4.9%) figures come from Lilly's topline release. A non-authoritative secondary site cited 6.5/11.2 vs 4.6% for discontinuations; this conflict is unresolved.
- (trials) The phase 2 NEJM paper, its supplementary appendix and the 2023 Lancet T2D paper were read from mirrored copies of the published PDFs (gwern.net), because nejm.org and thelancet.com blocked automated access. Editors should confirm those values against the publisher copies.
- (trials) The TRIUMPH-2 (Lancet) and TRANSCEND-T2D-1 (Lancet) full texts were not accessible (Cloudflare block); only the PubMed abstracts and Lilly releases were used. Per-arm heart rate, serious AEs and pancreatitis or gallbladder events from these papers are not captured.
- (trials) TRIUMPH-3 and TRIUMPH-4 are not yet peer reviewed as of 2026-10-07. All their numbers are company topline data. No ClinicalTrials.gov results have been posted for any phase 3 retatrutide trial.
- (trials) There are small enrollment discrepancies. TRIUMPH-1: 2,339 randomized (NEJM, Lilly) vs 2,335 actual enrollment (ClinicalTrials.gov). TRIUMPH-3: 1,949 (Lilly) vs 1,946 (ClinicalTrials.gov).
- (trials) Lilly's TRIUMPH-4 release lists NCT05869903, which is actually ATTAIN-1 (orforglipron). The correct TRIUMPH-4 ID is NCT05931367.
- (trials) The PubMed abstract of the TRIUMPH-1 NEJM paper prints the 12 mg hybrid-estimand sleep-apnea change as '31.7' with no minus sign. This is likely a typo, but it was not confirmed against the full text.
- (trials) Whether dysesthesia goes away after stopping retatrutide, what causes it, and why rates differed so much between trials (4.4% in TRANSCEND-T2D-1 vs 20.9% in TRIUMPH-4 at 12 mg) is not established in any source opened.
- (trials) The TRIUMPH-2 obstructive sleep apnea basket results, TRIUMPH-5 (vs tirzepatide), TRANSCEND-T2D-2 (vs semaglutide) and TRANSCEND-T2D-3 results were not yet reported as of 2026-10-07.
- (trials) Cardiovascular outcomes are unknown. TRIUMPH-Outcomes (NCT06383390, about 10,000 participants) has an estimated completion of 2029-02. The TRIUMPH-3 MACE analyses had too few events to be conclusive.
- (trials) No safety data beyond 104 weeks were found. No data were found on pregnancy outcomes, breastfeeding or people under 18. No data were found on people with prior pancreatitis, MTC/MEN-2, type 1 diabetes or prior bariatric surgery, because these groups were excluded.
- (trials) The phase 2 obesity trial's office pulse change at 48 weeks (supplement Figure S10A) could not be assigned to arms reliably from the extracted PDF figure labels, so it is not reported.
- (safety) Exact size of retatrutide's heart-rate increase (bpm) in phase 3: TRIUMPH-1 NEJM full text and supplement (reportedly Table S12/Fig S22) returned 403; phase 2 bpm values are only in the NEJM supplementary appendix, which was not opened. Only a pooled meta-analysis estimate (+3.46 bpm) was verified.
- (safety) Phase 3 retatrutide rates for gallbladder events, pancreatitis, hypoglycemia, arrhythmia, hair loss, injection-site reactions and kidney injury were not found in any opened source (company releases report only common AEs, dysesthesia, UTI and discontinuations; NEJM/Lancet full texts paywalled).
- (safety) Retatrutide half-life (relevant to how long overdose symptoms last) was not established from an opened primary source.
- (safety) No public rodent carcinogenicity (thyroid C-cell) data for retatrutide were found; thyroid risk statements rely on tirzepatide/semaglutide labels.
- (safety) Full TRIUMPH-1 (NCT05929066) exclusion criteria: the ClinicalTrials.gov text lists only weight change, prior weight-loss drugs, obesity surgery, diabetes, MTC/MEN-2 and pancreatitis. Whether heart rhythm disorders, depression/suicidality, kidney disease, gallbladder disease, gastroparesis, pregnancy/breastfeeding were phase 3 exclusions could not be confirmed (design paper Table S1 supplement download failed).
- (safety) Whether retatrutide reduces oral contraceptive effectiveness, and how long before pregnancy it should be stopped, is unknown (no label).
- (safety) Mechanism of dysesthesia/skin burning remains unknown; only hypotheses exist.
- (safety) No FDA/MHRA/NHS statement specifically addressing GLP-1 use in people with eating disorders was found; only peer-reviewed expert reviews.
- (safety) NHS medicine pages have been restructured (May 2026) and no longer include the older per-side-effect 'what to do about' advice; GI mitigation advice here comes from a peer-reviewed expert review instead.
- (safety) America's Poison Centers GLP-1 tracker data shown only through April 30, 2025; no retatrutide-specific poison center data found.
- (safety) No regulator source was found that specifically addresses melanotan risk in people with many moles or prior melanoma; TGA pages were read via Internet Archive copies because tga.gov.au timed out.
- (safety) The phase 2 NEJM retatrutide article text (arrhythmia, hypersensitivity, injection-site, chronotropic mechanism statements) was read from a non-publisher copy; those details are marked partial or noted.
- (safety) MHRA alert on diabetic ketoacidosis when insulin is rapidly reduced was seen only as a linked title; the bulletin itself was not opened.
- (safety) BMJ 2026 fact check on a reported death after retatrutide (PMID 42425580) was not opened (title only), so no claim was made about it.
- (gray) I found no source for the brief's line that vials 'ranged from about 16% under label to 49% over, with one containing no retatrutide'. None of the datasets I searched match it: the CBS, ABC, Drug and Alcohol Review, Leeder, TGA, Peptide Critic, Finnrick and JMIR data. The figure may be a garbled mix of several sources: ABC's 16.6% purity, the Drug and Alcohol Review vial at 51.3% of label (about 49% under), and the Leeder or TGA reports of a vial with no retatrutide. Recommend dropping or replacing it with the sourced per-vial numbers.
- (gray) I could not open the TGA alert (tga.gov.au timed out from here; WebFetch also timed out). The finding that a vial held semaglutide (about 8 times the normal level) and no retatrutide is marked unverified. Someone needs to read the alert on tga.gov.au directly.
- (gray) I did not open Lilly's six complaints (PACER/CourtListener). I cannot confirm whether any complaint includes Lilly's own lab tests of the defendants' products. Neither Lilly's release nor CBS mentions such tests, and I found no court rulings or settlements as of 2026-10-08.
- (gray) America's Poison Centers' own retatrutide exposure data (about 95 per month, +265%) is not public anywhere I could find. It is cited only through CBS News.
- (gray) Public Citizen's 'at least 14 hospitalizations' is not an FDA statement. My openFDA count gives 15 hospitalization reports from Jan 2025 to May 2026 and 16 through Jun 27, 2026. Public Citizen's '10 reports in 2025' differs from openFDA's 12. I did not query the FAERS Public Dashboard (a Qlik app) directly, so counts there may differ slightly by date field.
- (gray) WIRED's bodega test, which reportedly found no retatrutide in a Mr. Green vial tested through Finnrick, could not be checked because wired.com blocks this tool. I saw it only in non-citable reposts.
- (gray) Finnrick's per-vial measured mg, label mg, endotoxin and lab names are behind a login. Only one-line certificate summaries (purity %, fill %, identity) are public, and Finnrick's samples are not random. The Sept 24 cluster of 17 identity failures may deserve a caveat.
- (gray) The PekCura Labs warning letter (2026-03-31) names 'GLP-3-R', which is probably retatrutide under a code name, but the word 'retatrutide' never appears. Whether to count it is a judgment call: 21 letters versus 22.
- (gray) FDA Import Alert 66-80 (published 2026-09-21) has Green List entries whose importer description contains 'Retatrutide'. Their meaning (for example, legitimate clinical-supply imports) is unclear, so I left them out of the claims.
- (gray) I could not open DOJ primary documents for the Utah physician indictment (April 2026) or US v. Adam Taylor (M.D. Fla., Feb/Mar 2026), because justice.gov returned 403. These rest on CBS and Public Citizen.
- (gray) I could not find U.S. CBP seizure figures on cbp.gov. They come from Reuters via CNBC and cover all illicit GLP-1s, not retatrutide alone.
- (gray) I could not confirm whether ABC's 19 mg vial (June 27) is the same as sample 2 (19.0 mg) in the Drug and Alcohol Review study. If it is, it should not be counted twice in a chart.
- (gray) Lilly's investor site (investor.lilly.com) blocked automated access, so Lilly's release was read from Lilly's own PR Newswire distribution of the same text.
- (claims) Exact lean-mass loss (kg and % of total weight lost) per dose arm from the Lilly DXA body-composition substudy (Lancet Diabetes Endocrinol 2025) could not be read - full text paywalled; only the abstract and a secondary review (fat-loss index ~64.6%) were available. No phase 3 body-composition data were found.
- (claims) Whether retatrutide's glucagon component meaningfully raises calorie burn in humans is unknown: the human energy-expenditure study NCT06313528 completed in Aug 2025 but has no posted or published results (re-checked Oct 8, 2026).
- (claims) No head-to-head results vs tirzepatide (TRIUMPH-5, NCT06662383, estimated primary completion Nov 2026) or vs semaglutide (TRANSCEND-T2D-2) were available, so 'better than / fewer side effects than Ozempic or Mounjaro' cannot yet be tested directly.
- (claims) What happens to weight after stopping retatrutide is unknown: TRIUMPH-6 (NCT06859268) runs to about April 2028; only tirzepatide (SURMOUNT-4) data were used as an analogy.
- (claims) Long-term heart/kidney outcomes (TRIUMPH-Outcomes, ~2029) and liver outcomes (SYNERGY-Outcomes, ~2030) are not known; TRIUMPH-3 event counts were small and came from a company press release.
- (claims) Exact heart-rate increases (beats per minute per arm) from the phase 2 NEJM paper and the phase 3 NEJM/Lancet papers were not extracted because full texts were paywalled; only abstract-level statements and a meta-analysis figure were used.
- (claims) Per-arm rates of rarer serious events (pancreatitis, gallbladder disease, arrhythmia) in the phase 3 trials were not available from the abstracts or press releases opened.
- (claims) Whether retatrutide-associated dysesthesia goes away after stopping, and why it happens, was not established from the sources opened.
- (claims) The contaminant suspected in the Victorian acute liver-injury cases has not been identified in the health alert; news reports of a seventh case were not confirmed from a primary source.
- (claims) No systematic sterility or endotoxin testing of gray-market retatrutide vials was found; Finnrick data are consumer-submitted (not a random market sample) and the Australian peer-reviewed study tested only three vials.
- (claims) FDA adverse event (FAERS) counts were taken from Public Citizen's analysis (through May 2026), not directly from the FAERS public dashboard; more recent counts were not checked.
- (claims) The tga.gov.au advisory page timed out on direct access; its text was read via the Internet Archive copy of the exact official URL.
- (claims) No peer-reviewed content analysis of retatrutide claims on TikTok/Instagram/YouTube was found; claims were identified via Public Citizen's report and the Victorian health alert, not a systematic social-media survey.
- (claims) Reported Lilly-FDA dispute over whether retatrutide is regulated as a drug or a biologic (and any court appeal) could not be confirmed from a primary court or FDA document, so it was not included.
- (claims) Lilly's March 2026 TRANSCEND-T2D-1 topline (type 2 diabetes trial) was seen only in secondary coverage and was not opened or used.
- (catalog) No FDA final action was found after the July 23-24, 2026 PCAC votes. A Federal Register API search for 2026 found no proposed or final rule adding BPC-157, KPV, TB-500, MOTS-c, epitalon or semax to the 503A bulks list as of 2026-10-08. FDA has not posted official minutes on the meeting page, so the vote counts come from ABC News and Healio.
- (catalog) FDA has not set an exact date for the PCAC meeting planned 'before the end of February 2027' (GHK-Cu, melanotan II, LL-37, dihexa, PEG-MGF).
- (catalog) I could not retrieve the WADA 2027 Prohibited List (reportedly approved September 10, 2026, effective January 1, 2027). WADA's site still served the 2026 List, which is what the claims cite. Any 2027 changes to peptide entries are unverified.
- (catalog) I could not open the Russian State Register of Medicines (grls.rosminzdrav.ru). Semax's Russian registration rests on FDA's 2026 briefing document. Selank's rests only on a 2022 review table, which is why it is marked partial.
- (catalog) I did not find a regulator source that explicitly confirms GHK-Cu has no drug approval anywhere. It is clearly used as a cosmetic ingredient.
- (catalog) FDA's current safety-risk page no longer shows the original dates when BPC-157, TB-500, melanotan II and other withdrawn peptides entered Category 2 (only RAPS's 'in 2023'), or the exact date each nomination was withdrawn. The Wayback Machine was rate-limited.
- (catalog) Sermorelin: I did not verify whether any generic (ANDA) sermorelin has been approved since FDA's 2013 determination, or its current 503A/503B compounding status. Tesamorelin's compounding status was also not checked.
- (catalog) CagriSema's FDA decision was expected in Q4 2026 per Novo Nordisk (Sept 30, 2026) and was not yet public as of 2026-10-08.
- (catalog) The thymosin alpha-1 country approvals come from a 2014 SciClone annual report cited by FDA. I did not verify the current country-by-country status.
- (catalog) Melanotan II warnings from Ireland's HPRA (404) and Australia's TGA (timeout) could not be opened, and I did not locate FDA's 2007 Melanocorp action as a primary document.
- (catalog) The death of a subject in the 2006 CJC-1295 phase 2 trial and the deaths in the IV ipamorelin study are reported by FDA without establishing that the drug caused them.
- (catalog) I opened the Wegovy pill approval announcement as a syndicated copy (placera.se) of Novo Nordisk's 22 December 2025 company announcement, not on novonordisk.com. The DailyMed label confirms the tablets are approved.
- (catalog) No independent testing-lab data (e.g., Finnrick Analytics) on the purity or identity of gray-market versions of these peptides was collected for this catalog topic.
- (gap-phase3-serious-ae-rates-and-heart-rate) TRIUMPH-2 per-arm adjudicated pancreatitis, injection-site reactions, severe/serious hypersensitivity and acute renal events are presumably in the main Lancet article (DOI 10.1016/S0140-6736(26)01861-1). That article is paywalled and ScienceDrect/thelancet.com are bot-blocked. The free appendix and Lilly's EASD slides do not give them.
- (gap-phase3-serious-ae-rates-and-heart-rate) Any-severity gallbladder events broken out as cholelithiasis, cholecystitis or cholecystectomy are not reported per arm for TRIUMPH-1 or TRIUMPH-2; both publish only 'severe or serious gallbladder/biliary' counts. Only TRANSCEND-T2D-1 gives any-severity cholelithiasis.
- (gap-phase3-serious-ae-rates-and-heart-rate) Hair loss (alopecia) rates are not published for any phase 3 retatrutide trial. The only established fact is that it was below the ≥5% (TRIUMPH-1 and TRIUMPH-2) and ≥10% (NEJM Table 3) reporting thresholds.
- (gap-phase3-serious-ae-rates-and-heart-rate) TRIUMPH-1 (no diabetes) reports no hypoglycemia rate anywhere in the NEJM paper or appendix.
- (gap-phase3-serious-ae-rates-and-heart-rate) Exact peak heart-rate values are not printed in any source. The approximately 8.4 bpm (TRIUMPH-1, 12 mg, week 20) and approximately 5.7 bpm (TRIUMPH-2, 12 mg, week 20) figures are chart readings; only end-of-trial and follow-up values are printed.
- (gap-phase3-serious-ae-rates-and-heart-rate) The NEJM TRIUMPH-1 paper contradicts itself on 12 mg AE discontinuation: 11.1% in the text vs 65 (11.2) in Table 3. My guess is a denominator difference, but the paper does not say.
- (gap-phase3-serious-ae-rates-and-heart-rate) Inferring that Lilly's 4.9/4.1/6.9/11.3% figures count deaths as AE discontinuations rests on arithmetic for TRIUMPH-1. Only the TRIUMPH-2 slides state this rule explicitly.
- (gap-phase3-serious-ae-rates-and-heart-rate) Any-severity arrhythmia or palpitation rates for TRIUMPH-1 and TRIUMPH-2 are not published (only severe/serious); TRANSCEND-T2D-1 gives any-severity counts.
- (gap-phase3-serious-ae-rates-and-heart-rate) The TRIUMPH-1 'acute renal event' category is not broken down by preferred term, so I could not confirm that these were all acute kidney injury.
- (gap-phase3-serious-ae-rates-and-heart-rate) TRIUMPH-3 and TRIUMPH-4 detailed safety tables (pancreatitis, gallbladder, heart rate) are not yet peer-reviewed or publicly posted. ClinicalTrials.gov results are not posted for any phase 3 retatrutide trial as of 2026-10-08.
- (gap-phase3-serious-ae-rates-and-heart-rate) Lilly's Q3 2026 earnings deck (call scheduled 29 Oct 2026) and ObesityWeek 2026 materials do not exist yet and could not be checked.
- (gap-pk-peak-and-steady-state-primary) No published retatrutide source reports a measured accumulation ratio or a measured time to steady state. The only retatrutide-specific '4 to 5 weeks' is a 2020 Lilly protocol prediction. The only build-up evidence is the 3 mg fixed arm (Day 1 vs Week 12 AUC 33,800 → 70,100 ng·h/mL, about 2x by my calculation).
- (gap-pk-peak-and-steady-state-primary) Results of the injection-site study NCT05959096 (thigh and upper arm vs abdomen, plus an IV arm for absolute bioavailability) were not posted on ClinicalTrials.gov as of 2026-10-08, and no paper or congress poster was found. Retatrutide's site-specific exposure and absolute bioavailability remain unknown.
- (gap-pk-peak-and-steady-state-primary) No population-PK publication or ASCPT/ACCP/ADA/EASD clinical-pharmacology poster for retatrutide was found. The Clinical Pharmacology & Therapeutics and Journal of Clinical Pharmacology abstract supplements could not be searched directly, so a poster may exist that web and PubMed indexing missed.
- (gap-pk-peak-and-steady-state-primary) Completed retatrutide PK studies have no posted results: renal impairment (NCT05611957), hepatic impairment (NCT05916560), Japanese (NCT04823208), Chinese (NCT05548231), 14C mass balance (NCT05757531), drug interactions (NCT05445232, NCT06808802) and device bridging (NCT06003465).
- (gap-pk-peak-and-steady-state-primary) The Urva Lancet 2022 main text was read from a PDF copy of the typeset article hosted by sio-obesita.org, because the Lancet site is paywalled. The abstract was confirmed on PubMed, and the appendix was read from Elsevier's own CDN.
- (gap-pk-peak-and-steady-state-primary) Retatrutide has no FDA label (Lilly FAQ, September 2026), so there is no official section 12.3 with Tmax, steady-state or injection-site statements. A web-search summary suggested Lilly's 2026 Q2 earnings release mentions a US filing in early 2027. I did not open that filing, so it is not claimed here.
- (gap-pk-peak-and-steady-state-primary) The brief's timeline figures need correcting. 'Peak ~2-3 days' is not supported: medians are 12–72 h after a single dose and 12–48 h with repeat dosing (about 12–24 h by week 12). '12-49 h' should read 12–48 h. 'Steady state ~4-5 weekly doses' should be labelled as a sponsor prediction or class inference, not an observed result.
- (gap-pk-peak-and-steady-state-primary) The 4-user study (Carneiro 2026) estimated a half-life of 5.5–9.6 days (mean about 7.5). That is slightly longer than Lilly's approximately 6 days. This small, semi-quantitative study of non-Lilly material cannot overturn the trial value, but it shows that detection, and possibly effects, can persist for 8 or more weeks.
- (gap-side-effect-mechanism-and-mitigation) Retatrutide-specific dysesthesia timing: no accessible source gives onset week, median duration, share unresolved at trial end, or what happened after dose reduction or stopping. Lilly says only that the 'majority resolved during treatment'. The NEJM (TRIUMPH-1) and Lancet (TRIUMPH-2) full texts and the NEJM appendix returned HTTP 403, so the timing data used here come from semaglutide 7.2 mg (FDA label, EMA CHMP report).
- (gap-side-effect-mechanism-and-mitigation) TRIUMPH protocols: the published design paper (Giblin et al.) allows dose reduction only for GI effects or poor intake and never mentions dysesthesia. Whether investigators managed skin burning by cutting or pausing the dose is not public.
- (gap-side-effect-mechanism-and-mitigation) UTI mechanism on retatrutide: no proposed mechanism could be verified. The only relevant item, a 2026 Eur J Intern Med letter by Koufakis et al. titled 'Is the answer hidden in timing?', had no abstract and its full text was paywalled. The signal is also inconsistent: in TRIUMPH-2 placebo was 6.6% versus 3.8% and 6.3% at the two lower doses.
- (gap-side-effect-mechanism-and-mitigation) FDA review documents for Wegovy 7.2 mg (multidiscipline or clinical review) were not located. The US label text and the EMA CHMP report were used instead.
- (gap-side-effect-mechanism-and-mitigation) Retatrutide phase 3 rates for fatigue, headache, dizziness, hair loss and injection-site reactions were not reported in the Lilly releases or the abstracts that could be opened. The only retatrutide data for fatigue, dizziness and headache come from the phase 2 ClinicalTrials.gov results (NCT04881760). Those are structured JSON, so their quotes are only partial.
- (gap-side-effect-mechanism-and-mitigation) No authoritative source gives a confirmed mechanism for fatigue or headache with GLP-1-class drugs. The claims offer possible contributors: dehydration from GI losses, low blood sugar when combined with insulin or sulfonylureas, and lower iron or nutrient intake. These are flagged 'partial'.
- (gap-side-effect-mechanism-and-mitigation) Hypotension mitigation: no FDA label explicitly tells prescribers to review or reduce blood-pressure medicines. The labels say only that hypotension is more common with these medicines. The advice to change medicines comes from the general NHS hypotension page.
- (gap-side-effect-mechanism-and-mitigation) Several general NHS symptom pages (low blood pressure, dizziness, tiredness) are past their stated next-review dates, so they should be re-checked before display.
- (gap-side-effect-mechanism-and-mitigation) No AAD publication date was visible on its hair-shedding page. The AAD's 2-month and 4-month onset figures refer to postpartum shedding, not to weight loss specifically.
- (gap-red-flags-us-911-split) No US source gives a red-flag rule written for retatrutide. It has no FDA label or Medication Guide, so every tier here is borrowed from approved class labels (Zepbound, Wegovy) and general MedlinePlus, NIH, AHA, ADA, and CDC guidance.
- (gap-red-flags-us-911-split) Neither current Medication Guide (Zepbound 08/2026, Wegovy 06/2026) uses the phrase 'get emergency help' or says '911' for any side effect. They use 'get medical help right away' for allergy signs and 'call your healthcare provider right away' for pancreatitis and dehydration. The 911 tier rests on MedlinePlus, AHA, ADA, and CDC general pages.
- (gap-red-flags-us-911-split) Heart rhythm: no US source found gives one number that separates 911 from 'doctor today' for a racing heart alone. The split used here comes from MedlinePlus: palpitations plus chest pain, fainting, shortness of breath, sweating, or dizziness means 911, while a resting pulse over 100 without exercise, anxiety, or fever means contact a provider right away. The Wegovy guide says 'racing or pounding... lasts for several minutes', tell your provider. Zepbound's guide has no separate heart-rate warning.
- (gap-red-flags-us-911-split) Retatrutide heart-rate size per dose arm (bpm) was not collected. Only the phase 2 abstract statement (dose-dependent, peaking at 24 weeks) was verified. The NEJM full text or supplement and the TRIUMPH phase 3 data were not opened.
- (gap-red-flags-us-911-split) Pancreatitis and gallbladder: no US source found that says to call 911 for these specifically. The labels and NIDDK say 'right away' (doctor or seek care). MedlinePlus Acute pancreatitis only says 'contact your provider'. 911 applies only through the general abdominal-pain criteria (sudden sharp pain, rigid belly, vomiting blood, trouble breathing). These sources set different urgency levels for the same symptoms.
- (gap-red-flags-us-911-split) Possible conflict: the MedlinePlus Breathing difficulty page puts chest discomfort with breathing difficulty, and shortness of breath at rest, under 'See your provider', while the MedlinePlus Chest pain page puts crushing chest pressure with shortness of breath under 'Call 911'. The app may need to say that new chest pressure always goes to 911.
- (gap-red-flags-us-911-split) heart.org returned 403 to direct requests. The AHA quotes come from Internet Archive snapshots (Sep and Oct 2026 for tachycardia, heart attack, arrhythmia symptoms, and cardiac arrest; 2025-08-25 for the AFib page). The CDC heart attack page blocked curl and was read with the WebFetch summarizer, so its exact wording should be rechecked.
- (gap-red-flags-us-911-split) The ADA hypoglycemia page has no review date.
- (gap-red-flags-us-911-split) NIDDK pancreatitis and gallstones pages were last reviewed in November 2017 and the NIDDK hypoglycemia page in July 2021. They are still live, but they are old.
- (gap-red-flags-us-911-split) America's Poison Centers' own pages on GLP-1 exposures were not opened. The Poison Help number comes from the FDA-approved Medication Guides and MedlinePlus.
- (gap-personal-history-trial-exclusions) Full TRIUMPH-1 (J1I-MC-GZBJ) protocol could not be opened. The NEJM protocol/supplement (doi:10.1056/NEJMoa2604169) returned HTTP 403, and the EU CTIS record 2023-503657-35-00 returned an empty public record. So TRIUMPH-1's PHQ-9/C-SSRS cut-offs, gastroparesis exclusion, pregnancy/breastfeeding wording and contraception rules are unconfirmed; they are likely the same as TRIUMPH-2/3, but unverified.
- (gap-personal-history-trial-exclusions) Diabetic retinopathy: no visible phase 3 exclusion. TRIUMPH-2 exclusion #15 and TRIUMPH-3 exclusions #13–18 are redacted in the public CTIS protocols, and the Lancet TRIUMPH-2 full text/appendix returned HTTP 403. Whether proliferative retinopathy was excluded remains unknown.
- (gap-personal-history-trial-exclusions) Exact eGFR cut-off is redacted in the public protocols. The <30 mL/min/1.73 m² value comes only from design-paper Table S1.
- (gap-personal-history-trial-exclusions) Heart rhythm/QT: no arrhythmia or QTc exclusion appears in the visible TRIUMPH-2/3 criteria or in Table S1. Redacted criteria (#41–42 in TRIUMPH-2; #43–44 in TRIUMPH-3) and the TRIUMPH-1 protocol could not be checked.
- (gap-personal-history-trial-exclusions) Gallbladder disease history: no exclusion is visible, but absence is not proven because of the redactions and the missing TRIUMPH-1 protocol.
- (gap-personal-history-trial-exclusions) Oral contraceptives: the protocols require 2 methods (pill counts as highly effective), but no retatrutide–oral contraceptive interaction data or specific pill warning was found in the protocols. Whether a dedicated drug-interaction study exists was not established here.
- (gap-personal-history-trial-exclusions) The list of 'medications associated with significant weight gain' (e.g., which psychiatric drugs) is redacted (TRIUMPH-2 criterion #42; TRIUMPH-3 #44).
- (gap-personal-history-trial-exclusions) Mild-to-moderate heart failure (NYHA I–III): not excluded per visible criteria, but the number of such participants actually enrolled is not reported in the sources read.
- (gap-surgery-pregnancy-practical-guidance) Results of Lilly's retatrutide + combined oral contraceptive study (NCT06039826; completed 2024-07-11, 46 participants) are not posted on ClinicalTrials.gov, and no publication was found, so whether retatrutide reduces pill hormone levels is publicly unknown.
- (gap-surgery-pregnancy-practical-guidance) The primary retatrutide gastric-emptying paper (Urva et al., Diabetes Obes Metab 2023;25:2784-2788, PMID 37311727) could not be opened (no abstract, paywalled, Wiley returned 403). Its size and duration of effect are known only through a 2026 review.
- (gap-surgery-pregnancy-practical-guidance) No data show how long after stopping retatrutide the stomach returns to normal. Any surgery timeline is a half-life inference, and sources warn that half-life can't confirm gastric recovery.
- (gap-surgery-pregnancy-practical-guidance) Phase 3 TRIUMPH/TRANSCEND protocols are not public, so it's unknown whether they kept the phase 2 rule of contraception for 2 months (women) and 4 months (men) after the last injection.
- (gap-surgery-pregnancy-practical-guidance) The contraception appendix pages of the phase 2 obesity protocol PDF (NCT04881760) were image-only and unreadable. The method table was taken from the phase 2 T2D protocol (NCT04867785) instead.
- (gap-surgery-pregnancy-practical-guidance) Regulators disagree on tirzepatide and the pill: the EU SmPC says the effect is not clinically relevant, while FDA and MHRA say to use backup or non-oral contraception. Whether the EU text has since been revised was not checked beyond the PDF downloaded on 2026-10-08.
- (gap-surgery-pregnancy-practical-guidance) The TGA retatrutide safety alert (Sept 2026) could not be opened (timeout), so whether it says anything about pregnancy or surgery is unverified.
- (gap-surgery-pregnancy-practical-guidance) ASA's own October 2024 news release was not opened (guessed URL returned 404). The ASA position was taken from the journal versions and the ASA 2023 page's update note.
- (gap-surgery-pregnancy-practical-guidance) The web search budget was used up mid-task, so a final check for any 2026 ASA, AGA or ASGE update, or an EMA statement specific to retatrutide or triple agonists, was not done.
- (gap-surgery-pregnancy-practical-guidance) The antidoping study's ~56-day detection window comes from only 4 self-injecting users of unverified products. It shows the drug can be detected, not that it is still having an effect.
- (gap-gray-contaminant-sterility-data) What contaminant caused the Victorian liver-injury cluster is still unknown. As of 2026-10-08, no allowed source names one. The Victorian alert has not been updated since 19 June 2026. Dr Sinclair's analysis by two labs ('we don't have the full picture') has not been published. The Austin Hospital vial-testing results have not been released.
- (gap-gray-contaminant-sterility-data) The seventh Victorian case is reported only by ABC News (24 Aug 2026, quoting Dr Marie Sinclair). No updated Victorian Department of Health alert or other primary document confirming seven cases was found.
- (gap-gray-contaminant-sterility-data) No peer-reviewed case series or case report of the Victorian or ACT retatrutide liver injuries was found in PubMed or Europe PMC (checked 2026-10-08).
- (gap-gray-contaminant-sterility-data) No retatrutide-specific sterility (germ) or endotoxin data exists from any allowed source. The only peer-reviewed retatrutide vial analysis (Drug Alcohol Rev 2026, n=3) explicitly did not test them. Finnrick's retatrutide endotoxin results sit behind login and are not aggregated. The Mendias & Awan preprint pools 14 peptides (15% endotoxin-positive) and its full text could not be accessed.
- (gap-gray-contaminant-sterility-data) Heavy-metal data specific to retatrutide comes from only three Australian vials (lead 0.7 mg/kg, other metals below detection limits). No US or other-country retatrutide heavy-metal data was found.
- (gap-gray-contaminant-sterility-data) The TGA has published only one retatrutide lab test: a vial that held semaglutide instead, about 8x the approved strength. It has published no testing of liver-cluster vials. Its adverse-event database (DAEN) was not checked because it requires accepting terms of use.
- (gap-gray-contaminant-sterility-data) The America's Poison Centers figure of about 95 retatrutide exposures per month (+265%) could not be traced to any APC publication. APC's public GLP-1 page has data only to 30 April 2025 and no retatrutide breakdown, and the CBS article itself was not opened.
- (gap-gray-contaminant-sterility-data) Leeder Analytical's 18-sample retatrutide data (1/18 matching the label) and its estimate that about 10% of vial contents is peptide are known only through ABC News. The lab's own publication was not located.
- (gap-gray-contaminant-sterility-data) It is unconfirmed whether the ABC June 2026 vial (19 mg, 16.6% purity) is the same vial as sample 2 (19.0 mg) in the Drug and Alcohol Review letter.
- (gap-gray-contaminant-sterility-data) Substack and newsletter claims that could not be traced to a primary source were excluded: 'low levels of heavy metals and endotoxins', 'endotoxin detected in every sample' and '7.7–14.4% purity'.
- (gap-gray-contaminant-sterility-data) Official alerts from NSW, Queensland and ACT health departments were not checked. The web-search budget (200 per turn) ran out mid-task, so newer Sept–Oct 2026 updates may exist and a follow-up search is recommended.
- (protect-dispose) dea.gov/takebackday, the DEA 2026 press release, and justthinktwice.gov all returned HTTP 403 to curl and WebFetch. So I could not confirm from DEA what Take Back Day does NOT accept. Secondary sources say needles, sharps, and illegal drugs are not accepted, but this is unverified. A human should check dea.gov/takebackday before the site says needles can't go to Take Back Day.
- (protect-dispose) FDA's 2015 Drug Safety Communication on not sharing multi-dose pens returned 404 at both its current and old URLs. I used the 2026 Zepbound label instead. Confirm whether FDA archived or replaced that communication.
- (protect-dispose) The CDC HIV Risk Reduction Tool page has a banner saying it was restored to its 2025-01-29 version under a court order and may be changed or removed. The main www.cdc.gov/hiv/causes page was only reachable through WebFetch's summarizer, not as raw text. Re-check that both are still live before publishing.
- (protect-dispose) Retatrutide is not FDA-approved, so it has no official label or disposal instructions. Claims 11-20 are general FDA/CDC rules for needles, syringes, and pens. Present them that way, not as product-specific guidance.
- (protect-dispose) Is any injectable GLP-1/GIP peptide (pen or vial) on FDA's Flush List? I did not check. Product-specific disposal steps for pens and vials sit in Instructions for Use, which also contain handling and usage content that is out of scope for this agent.
- (protect-dispose) I did not collect non-US guidance (MHRA, TGA, Health Canada) on take-back programs and sharps disposal. Should international readers get country-specific claims?
- (protect-dispose) The Safe Needle Disposal hotline (1-800-643-1643) is run by NeedyMeds, a nonprofit. FDA and EPA reference it, but it is not itself a regulator. Decide whether the site should show it directly.
- (protect-real) TGA (tga.gov.au) timed out on every attempt from this environment, so no TGA claim is included. Search snippets suggest TGA has said 'research use only' disclaimers don't change a product's legal status, and that a tested 'retatrutide' product contained undeclared semaglutide and no retatrutide. Neither page was opened, so both are unverified.
- (protect-real) HRSA's poisonhelp.hrsa.gov returned 403 to both curl and WebFetch. Poison Help and 911 guidance comes from NIH MedlinePlus instead. Re-check HRSA if a primary Poison Help source is preferred.
- (protect-real) The full AAP 2023 obesity guideline (publications.aap.org) was blocked by a bot check, so only AAP's HealthyChildren.org summary was used. No FDA or AAP statement was found aimed specifically at teens buying weight-loss injections online or from social media.
- (protect-real) MHRA's 'powder in vials = not authorised' (protect-real-17) is UK-only. In the U.S., FDA acknowledges that state-licensed pharmacies may dispense compounded GLP-1s in multi-dose vials. The site should not tell U.S. readers that any vial means fake.
- (protect-real) The relayed user request asks, for each peptide, for dosing, appearance, refrigeration/storage, handling and treatment length. Per the task constraints, none of that was collected. FDA's GLP-1 page has storage and vial-use details, and the Wegovy label has dosing; both were deliberately left out. The orchestrator should tell the user that a kids/teens education site about unapproved peptides should not carry how-to dosing or storage content. The 'real vs fake' and 'get help' facts above can fill that click-through panel instead.
- (protect-real) Lilly's investor press release on the retatrutide black market (investor.lilly.com) returned Access Denied. Lilly claims rely on the lilly.com FAQ page instead, which was opened and quoted.
